# Muster 2 — closed cards (register of record)

Rows moved verbatim from `MUSTER_2_CARDS.md` when their status became
DONE / CLOSED / STALE / SUPERSEDED / RETIRED / WITHDRAWN / MOOT (first cut
2026-09-07, Arthur: the live register must fit one read). Same columns;
a closed card is never edited here — a re-opened one goes back to the
live register with a new status line.

| C415 | ops | Arthur 2026-09-22, ruling 329, raised 2026-09-22 | **Jobs are not services, and nothing records that one ran.** Seven live jobs (measured from the call ledger, not from what looks live) keep concurrency and context in FOUR homes — module literals, call-site literals, env vars, one YAML with a single entry — and FIVE of the seven have no unit, so no record exists that a run happened at all. Build: one job registry (definition), one append-only RUN ledger (args, card, served model, counts in/out, outcome, error), and the scheduler ruling 329 requires — place the model, reconfigure the card, evict. 7 job types against 4 cards means a queue and an eviction order; reviewer is 77% of all calls so that order decides how long everything else waits. | Claude-fix | L | **CLOSED 2026-09-22 — the executor, and the jobs can keep a history.** `control.py`: placement is a systemd DROP-IN so the unit file stays the record and a reversal is one unlink; four guards — dry-run unless armed, never restart a card with work in flight (330), ledger BEFORE the action, never install/enable/retire. `runjob.py`: the run is the PROCESS, wrapped in `ExecStart`, because a hook in modelgate would invert the layering AND miss the runs worth having (a job that dies before its first call). Exit code passes through unchanged, signals forwarded, an unregistered job type recorded and RUN. Attribution is exact: the wrapper sets `FLEET_RUN_ID`, modelgate stamps it on every call row, and the row says which basis it used — a time window cannot tell two concurrent runs of one job type apart, and second-truncated timestamps made every short run read as zero calls. Proven end to end on production code (run_id stamped, calls=1, model named). 163 tests, 49 of 49 mutants across three passes. NOT armed, on purpose: nothing calls `apply()` on a clock (the first swap should be watched) and no unit is wrapped (an ExecStart change is Arthur's). Queue counters stay per-service work. |

| C083 | factory | homed (critic 10) | Delete-replace register feeds not verified for reader isolation under concurrent timers | Claude-fix | M | CLOSED 2026-10-07 22:4x — UNVERIFIED, not unsound. The 09-19 triage read the code's SHAPE ('no transaction-isolation or savepoint structure') and concluded from an absence; the ENGINE was never asked. **`TRUNCATE` is transactional and takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE**, so a reader mid-derive BLOCKS and then sees the new rows — never zero, and a savepoint would add nothing. DEMONSTRATED against the real engine on a throwaway table (reader times out, then sees the new count) and the hazard shown by running the mutation: with `autocommit=True` the reader is served **0**. All four reloaders already hold ONE transaction. Gated: no commit between first and last TRUNCATE, no autocommit, and no two derives truncate the same table (the concurrent-timers half — 9 tables, no sharing, so no deadlock). 11 tests; registers 615 pass |

The checkpoint is stored WITH the count it was taken at and honoured only while that matches — the self-invalidation the residue rows already used. A closed year always resumes, the current year never does. Checkpoint written AFTER the page lands (a crash re-reads it, never skips it) and per DIRECTION (desc/asc start at opposite ends). Saves ~75 pages × 2-3 s per interrupted year. 6 tests on the live ledger. |

| C108 | factory | homed, homed | Syndication dedup untested beyond verbatim — shingle/minhash pass never run; every coverage number inherits it | Claude-fix | M | **CLOSED 2026-10-08 — it took thirteen seconds, and the verbatim hash had seen 52 of 943.** `ops/near_dup_scan.py`: 5-word shingles, 64 MinHash permutations, 16×4 banded LSH, then the EXACT Jaccard on every candidate (no new dependency — numpy was already here). **Q3 2026, 41,583 articles, 103 source labels:** J≥0.9 **943 pairs of which 52 byte-identical**, 629 cross-source near-only, **2.7% of the window's documents**; J≥0.8 2,067 pairs and 4.9%; J≥0.6 13,720 and 9.6%. What the corpus owed was an honest denominator, so the card's second half is discharged in the GENERATOR: `coverage_scan.NEAR_DUP_CAVEAT` prints in COVERAGE_2026-09.md's header — a record is one outlet's copy, not one story — where a regeneration cannot drop it. the board prints the verdict on the red's own line plus a REDS BY VERDICT summary. **It is not an exemption list**: a verdict labels a red and never silences one, nothing in the pass/fail path reads it, and a test greps the runner to prove that, alongside refusals for an UNTRIAGED red and for an ACTIONABLE verdict whose card has CLOSED. **The sixteen: 10 ACTIONABLE, 4 STANDING, 1 WRONG, and FOUR went green in the hour** — T7.24, T7.54, T7.22 and T7.13 — leaving the board at **13 red of 67, every one verdicted** (`9 ACTIONABLE · 3 STANDING · 1 WRONG`). **THREE WERE THE BOARD BEING OUT OF DATE, not the estate being broken** — T7.24 reds because `querynz-vision-ocr` has no DSN and it runs `llama-server`, which cannot read one, added after that arm's exempt list was written; The helper is now the leaf `ingestion/browser_profile.py` (imports `os` and `pathlib`, nothing else) which both layers reach without either reaching into the other — the `estate_dsn` precedent — and hansard re-exports it under the names its own suite asserts. **And C528's SECOND finding was the load-bearing one**: clearing the lock was necessary and not sufficient, the launch still died in 632 ms, and moving the whole **229 MB** profile aside let the page read on the first try. So the lane recovers itself — clear a dead owner's lock (a LIVE owner still raises, because two Chromiums on one profile is what corrupts it), launch, and on failure RETIRE the profile and retry **once**; a second failure raises, since a retry loop would thrash a quarter of a gigabyte against a host that is merely refusing us. The retirement is a move, never a delete, and a previous `.broken` is replaced so two copies is the ceiling. **Proven twice:** the real profile → `Result=success` after 35 consecutive failures; then broken deliberately (`chmod 000 Default`) → the journal reads *"launch failed (TargetClosedError); retired a profile…"* and two pages read, 69,256 and 1,833 chars, success. Fixed: `sys.path.insert(0, _REPO)`, and both swallows now append to `STOP_CONSULT_ERRORS`, which T7.19 prints — a check that cannot run is not a check that said no. Every line was correct; the body is cached 5 s (A39) and the counter sat INSIDE it, so it served what had been true before the flood — and the probe has its own 30/min budget, so during a real flood the probe is refused too and the stale body is all a reader gets. `rl_window()` is a dict read: it is now computed on the way out of BOTH health paths and stored in neither. Re-proven live — 46 refusals read back **3 s later, inside the TTL**, as `total: 46, top: [127.0.0.x 46]`. **(4) The board line**: T7.12 probed `/api/health` and discarded every body (`-o /dev/null`), so the number had a counter and no reader for a month. It prints `PI 429s in 24 h: 46 (127.0.0.x 46)` in the same informational `tail` the parked sources use, and **cannot contribute to `bad`** — an unreachable product is already T7.12's probe failure and T7.33's, and a second voice would double-count one fault. **A number, not a threshold**: re-measured at the close with no override in the serving environment, the limits are 120/min sustained and burst 40 against a page load of ~5 calls, so a real browser never meets the limiter and only an unpaced runner does. `since` is gone from the payload. | C215 | sources | homed | times.co.nz / stoppress Cloudflare-headless depth: intra-sitemap checkpointing in run_source | Claude-fix | S | **CLOSED 2026-10-08 — a refusal recorded as thin content, and a batch that could not survive its slice. Eight polite minutes a day against a real publisher, for nothing, reported as success (#51242b, with `rc 0` as what made it read as done). **Cause: `fetch_all` was ATOMIC** over the whole discovered set — up to `--limit 1500` browser renders at ~5 s each — so the 8-minute cap killed it mid-render and nothing banked. Now chunked: a module declares `FETCH_CHUNK` and the batch renders+LANDS that many at a time, so a killed run keeps every completed chunk and the next run's pre-fetch dedup skips them (ruling 28's own measure of progress). **OPT-IN** because nbr/businessdesk log in once per `fetch_all` call — chunking them would be thirty logins in eight minutes against a paywall. Proven: a bounded stoppress walk went from landing nothing to `fetched 20, landed 1`. **AND THAT RUN EXPOSED THE REAL DEFECT — `thin: 19` of 20.** One page renders to 3,834 chars; six in a batch give 258 KB first and **~29 KB with zero text** after. It is Cloudflare's **"Just a moment..."** interstitial, 29,045 B. `render_all` broke out of its challenge wait `if _CHALLENGE_MARKER not in html or len(html) > 20_000` — **two holes in one line**: the marker was Incapsula's, and the size escape defeated the check even for the vendor it knew, because an interstitial is mostly inlined CSS while a short real article is small. The shell went to the extractor, returned nothing, and the lane called it **`thin`** — the publisher's fault, not the refusal it was. `is_challenge()` is vendor-agnostic now, the size escape is gone, a challenged page is **never returned as content** (named, counted, omitted, with a summary), and `ingestion/gets/sync.py`'s copy is repointed. **Verdict: stop asking.** A challenged read is a failed read, so `stoppress.co.nz` → **`blocked:antibot`**, which also drops it from `coverage_backwalk.plan` (ready-only), ending the daily slice — ruling 290's *the stop is the whole remedy*. robots PERMITS us (read 10-05): this is the WAF, not the permission surface, and nothing is worked around; on the last probe even page one was challenged, the host having tightened against repeated asking. **The card's two sources had OPPOSITE verdicts**: times.co.nz does not challenge (4 of 4 archive pages, 2,264–6,624 chars) and stays `ready` — it is the lane the chunking helps. **Left measured, not fixed:** `completed_subsitemaps` needs a sub-sitemap's PRE-dedup count inside `--limit`, and stoppress's first holds 1,000 urls (`limit_cut: 980`), so `done` can never fill and the census reads 0.0% however much lands; the honest fix needs per-sub url lists from the walker and is a second card. Also fixed: my own board line crashed on a non-dict health body (caught by `test_manifest_probe_parity`, which stubs `_sh` with `"200"`) — shape-checked, because an informational line must never take the unit watcher down. Lesson #8fee8d. 15 tests; ingestion + ops 1,494 passed Two more on the way out: `runnable()` answered a blocked-antibot source with *"needs browser fetch"*, which is the right remedy for a source with no browser lane and the WRONG one for stoppress, which has rendered through the headless browser since 2026-07-17 and is challenged anyway — it sent the next reader to redo what already failed, and now states that a challenged read is a failed read and the stop IS the remedy. And the board generator REFUSED TO RENDER (`CLUSTER archive-depth: C215 is not an open item`) because my close helper removes a triage entry but not cluster membership — the gate catching a disagreement between its own sources rather than publishing one. 17 tests |
| C408 | PI product | Arthur, corpus search, raised 2026-09-22 | **A one-word query finds 1% of its own subject.** `sandmining` returns 10 served documents, newest 2025-07-18; `sand mining` returns **1,398**, 15 of them in 2026. Same subject, 140x, decided by spelling: `websearch_to_tsquery('english', …)` makes the compound ONE lexeme (`sandmin`) and never decomposes it, and NZ outlets write it as two words. Corpus is NOT the problem — it is current to today (rnz/scoop latest 2026-09-22). Second finding, same surface: `SEARCHABLE_SQL` indexes title + **the first 1,500 characters only**, so a subject named below the lead is invisible at any spelling. SCOPE: measure how many one-word queries this hits before choosing a fix (a compound lexicon, a split-and-OR fallback, or a wider index are three different costs). | Claude-fix | M | **CLOSED 2026-10-08 — the lexicon disappeared when the index was allowed to score the guesses.** The card's scope was *measure before choosing a fix*, and the measurement killed one of its three options. **First, a stated limit: there is NO search query log**, so "how many one-word queries this hits" cannot be answered from real queries at all — the population is built from the corpus, which is a different question wearing the same words. **The compound defect is far worse than the card's example**: over 180 days of served articles, `socialmedia` 2 vs `social media` 2,306 = **1,153×**, `nationalparty` 1,091×, `superrugby` 864×, `mentalhealth` 842×, `winstonpeters` 339× — `sandmining`'s 140× was the MILD case. **The population, and why reading it mattered**: 254,144 adjacent pairs from 60,000 titles, top 4,000 tested, **301 carry a one-word form** — and reading them (#7d90b4) showed most are worthless (`forthe`, `andthe`, `fromthe`, `thenew` are run-together typos; `newzealand` is domain names). A curated lexicon would have had to carry all 301 plus the judgment about which matter, for ever. **So: no lexicon.** A single bare word is cut at every position leaving both halves ≥4 letters and the cuts are OR-ed in as PHRASE queries beside the reader's own spelling, which stays first. A nonsense cut (`ment <-> alhealth`) matches nothing, so **the index scores the guesses**. Measured live: `socialmedia` 2→**2,173**, `superrugby` 1→**821**, `mentalhealth` 1→**755**, `nationalparty` 3→**1,466**, `sandmining` 0→**5**; and **unchanged** for `forthe` (123→123, no valid cut at the floor) and `kiwifruit` (191→191, a real word keeps its own hits). **PHRASE not AND, worth 2×**: `national & party` matches 3,274 including "National" beside "third party"; `national <-> party` 1,466 — a compound means adjacency, and the looser operator buys recall with coincidence. **THE SECOND FINDING IS BIGGER AND SPELLING CANNOT EXPLAIN IT** → **C543**: the index covers title + the first 1,500 chars and **77.5% of served articles exceed it** (mean 4,147, max 54,584), so the search cannot reach `sand mining` 90.2%, `lobbying` 60.9%, `consent` 54.8%, `Fonterra` 46.6%, `donation` 45.2% of their own matches — a median near half, at every spelling. That is a GIN rebuild on the serving DB (2,751 MB over 47.8% of the text, so ~2.1× for whole-text), Arthur's under ruling 317, runsheet §AR. **And it was unsafe to apply until today**: `primitives`' own docstring warned index-status "cannot see the expression" and that warning WAS the protection — `index_status` read name and size, never `indexdef`. Editing the window would have left every query matching an expression the GIN index does not, sequential-scanning 7.3M rows **while every instrument reported ready**. `ready` now requires the expression to match, compared through postgres's rewriting; proven True live and False against both a 3,000-char and a whole-text window. **A new match rule must go where the match is DEFINED**: four surfaces each bound `params["q"]` after `_window`, so the fallback made `salience` disagree with `keyword` about one word — caught only because `test_salience_real` asserts the cells sum to `keyword.n`. The term goes through `_window` now and a test refuses any later binding. Two of my own defects caught by existing tests: a second query broke a fake that models one result set, and a split pinned to `qsplit2` when it is `qsplit4`. Lesson #c6a926. 15 tests; corpus_search + query + console 688 passed |
| C425 | PI product | C152, C165, Arthur 2026-09-23 | **Who received it, on every filed name.** Arthur: "i need to be able to see the reciever of the donations associated to the names in the identity tab." So the only thing left to buy was that a refactor cannot silently drop one: **13 tests**, two seen to FAIL when the group fold and its `<Received>` are removed, also pinning that the fold unions across spellings (one spelling giving where another did not is the whole evidence), merges on `(party, candidate)`, widens the year span rather than picking one, orders by money, **produces no group money total** (a group is a question about identity, not a settled donor), and that `receivers_by_name` is called exactly twice per request because it is a full-table read over three registers. **The card's ruling citation is loose** — it cites 192 for the no-summing rule and 192 is the mechanical fold widening; the behaviour is right either way and the test states its reason in its own words. **The group header showed 121 where a spelling showed 133**, which cannot happen to a union: per spelling the key is `(party, candidate, electorate, source)` and the fold's is `(party, candidate)`, collapsing a candidate who stood in two electorates (correct) and a party return against a disclosure (chased to `Rank Group Limited` → ACT: $200k of 2026 disclosures beside a $50k 2025 return — different money, so not a double-count here). That chase found genuine duplicates in `donation_disclosures` → **C544**, and **my first measurement of them was 26× too high**: keyed on (donor, party, year, amount) it said 11 keys and $752,300, but Christopher Reeve's two $50,000 to ACT were received 29 April and 18 September 2026 and Dame Jenny Gibbs's two were February and September 2023 — separate donations that look identical without the date. With the date and the sha the honest figure is **$28,800**. Noted, not carded: the endpoint takes 43 s at `limit=3`. 13 tests |
| C253 | factory | homed | Conjunction set: 18 parked two-people at 0.90 hand-read; 917 behind the 40/cycle cap | Claude-fix | S | **CLOSED 2026-10-08 — both numbers in the card were stale, and the lane the card worried about had drained.** The card: *"18 parked two-people at 0.90 hand-read; 917 behind the 40/cycle cap."* Measured live: the conjunction lane has quarantined **931** entities across six reason/confidence bands — `not-a-person` 571 at 0.95, 110 at 1.00, 99 at 0.90; `split` 126 at 0.95, 13 at 0.90, 12 at 1.00 — with the newest on **2026-10-06**, so the 40/cycle cap did NOT stall silently and read as done (#51242b checked, and the answer is the lane advanced). Nothing in the band needed reversing. **NEW FINDING, and it is a different job** → **C546**: 452 LIVE person entities still match `X and Y` exactly (2,235 contain `and`/`&` anywhere), and reading them shows they are predominantly **two REAL people needing a SPLIT, not a quarantine** — `Mallard and Hipkins`, `Holmes and Eckstein`, `Jones and Cave`, `George & Noriko`, `Raphael and Hugo` — with junk like `Bar and Restaurant` mixed in. That is why the lane left them: quarantining is cheap and splitting requires deciding WHO the two are. The card's asks are both answered; the residue is not the card's. Seen to FAIL first: it caught exactly `courts-extract` with 56 units passing. Since it rides the pre-push gate, a unit the MANIFEST declares disabled on **BOTH** boxes skips **with the unresolved imports in the skip reason** — reported every run, not exempted — and flipping `node2` to `enabled` makes it FAIL, which is proven. The skip reads INTENT from the manifest, never systemd, because an uninstalled unit is not a decision. **Second half done too**: three register derives (`disclosure`, `hansard`, `worksafe`) asked `INCOMING_NEWS_URL` before `V3_BACKLOG_DSN` — the order the 2026-08-22 standby incident indicted, when a unit's moved EnvironmentFile reached node2's read-only STANDBY and died on its first INSERT. FIXED: `is_seed()` (depth 0 or `sitemap` in url) is never held; `fold()` keys the held map on scheme-insensitive `www.`-stripped host+path; seeds deduped (robots often declares the guessed path); **the fuse now counts REQUESTS** — it counted held pages, so ACT's 4,125 held could never clear a 4,000 fuse once seeds were fresh. Fetch host and robots NEVER folded (two permission surfaces); landed labels stay C530's. Three instruments rejected by measurement: `found_on` out-degree (BFS order — labour/news scores 0), depth<=1 (sitemap-contaminated, 7,992 pages/run), sitemap `<lastmod>` (absent for ACT/Vision/Labour). LIVE PROOF: re-fired clean in 36m34s — **261 fetches against 320 and 189 never-held paths against 115** (ACT 80, Opportunity 48, Green 46, **Labour exactly the 8 the live diff predicted**); every party fetches something now except NZ Loyal (403 root) and Te Tai Tokerau (robots unreadable, refused); Women's Rights fetched 6, ALL seeds, 0 new — the designed cost, no invented work. **And three front-page parties blew the 4,000 fuse on HELD pages** (ACT 3915+85 with 4,445 left of an 8,517 frontier; Green 926 left; National 659 left), confirming the fuse defect in the same run that fixed discovery — `TimeoutStartUSec=5h` checked so the fix does not just move the stall. Cost moved to disk: 2.6 GB of stored-file reads against 261 requests. Ingest landed 196 articles. 35 tests; each regression direction bites alone (full revert 9, seed 7, fold 2, fuse 1), after a first version whose fold-keyed fixtures let the seed tests pass for the wrong reason; ops suite 1178 passed. Lesson #e125bd. Day-log 2026-10-08. |

A ceiling you cannot reach is a comment, not a control. FIXED on node1 and live: **High=36G, Max=39G** (38.4 < 39 < 40.7), derived from the 21 passes with the arithmetic in the unit. T7.65 now reads the JOURNAL's `Consumed …` peaks over 14 days instead of the live `MemoryPeak` systemd resets per run — it had never seen the 37.7 G pass that was over its own 85% line — **skips runs the OOM killer truncated**, and gained a check that a KILLING cap above MemAvailable is not a control at all. Also measured: `wal-archive-prune`, which the kills followed by 2–5 min, peaks at **32.4 MiB** — INNOCENT, the timing was coincidence and the squeeze is this job against postgres. T7.65 stays RED with a verdict, because 85% headroom needs 44.4 GiB and the box can spare 40.7: **the index no longer fits node1**, which is exactly the signal C435 said a tripped ceiling would be → C550, with node1's 20 uncapped units and vision-ocr's 12.4 G against its 6G cap. Lesson #c41887. |

Measured: records on **66 of 90 days**, Monday median 11.5 over counts 4–31, Wednesday median 2, **Thursday 6 days at median 1**, Saturday 3 days in the window. `classify` calls a source daily at ≥54 of 90 days, so a paper publishing in bursts is judged by a daily floor and its 24 empty days read as a degrading scraper. Needs: decide what `daily` MEANS, measured across all 143 source labels — how many sit near the 60% line and what each one's weekday spread is — then either a kind between `daily` and `weekly`, or a sparse rule that reads burstiness. Not a threshold nudged until an arm goes green. | Claude-fix | M | **CLOSED 2026-10-08 — the floor was a yardstick a weekday publisher cannot reach, and the degrading ones passed it.** Measured over the live 90-day window (100 labels with any article, 143 rows): a Mon–Fri source tops out at 5/7 = **0.714**, so the 0.60 all-days floor left it **16%** slack where a 7-day source got 40% — `gazette.govt.nz`, published on working days BY STATUTE, sat at 0.70 while delivering on **97%** of the days it works. And of the 25 sources that floor called `daily`, exactly **three** are under 0.80 on their own working days (`newsroom` 0.64, `businessdesk` 0.65, `labour` 0.75) — it passed the ones actually failing. The distribution is bimodal (34 labels under 0.1, 17 over 0.9, three in 0.6–0.7), so nothing turned on where one line fell. **FIXED, and no threshold was nudged:** `kind` is now the WEEKDAY PATTERN — a weekday counts as worked at ≥50% of its occurrences (the live profiles separate cleanly: gazette 13/13/13/12/12/0/0, hospitalitybusiness 12/13/6/5/2/1/6) and 7 worked days = `daily`, 5–6 `weekdays`, 2–4 `periodic`, 1 `weekly`. Sparseness is judged only on worked weekdays — closing the hole the 2026-10-05 per-weekday fix left, which covered a weekday NEVER used but not one used SOMETIMES (localmatters: 3 of 13 Saturdays, every other Saturday charged against those three) — and only where the floor reaches one article, because `0.25 × median` is not a quantity when the median is 1: **86 of the 222 (source, weekday) pairs have a median under 4** and 32 have exactly 1, so for 39% `sparse_frac` had no effect at all. `floor >= 1` is arithmetic, not a chosen number. **RESULT: sources over T7.43's 7-day ceiling fall from 16 to 3** — newsroom 26 and businessdesk 24 (the two measured at 0.64/0.65) and localmatters 9, whose remaining days are all on its HEAVY weekdays (four Tuesdays at 0–2 against a normal 11, three Sundays at 0–3 against a normal 15) — a real volume signal, not its rhythm. gazette 28→0, labour→0, hospitalitybusiness→0, beehive 3. 14 tests, each of the three halves independently gated (kind 3, worked-weekday 1, floor 2); one existing test asserted `daily` for the Mon–Fri source and was updated with the reason. COVERAGE's legend now states the rule. **T7.43 IS GREEN** — red since 2026-09-15, 38 consecutive boards. The three still over the raw ceiling are all accounted for without touching it: newsroom and businessdesk are `blocked:credentials`, so ruling 290 excludes them (the stop IS the remedy), and localmatters' 9 includes 5 estate days charged to our own downtime, leaving 4. T7.43's ACTIONABLE verdict was therefore REMOVED, not re-pointed. Board reds 13 → 12. Lesson #4d0a9d. |

| C532 | factory | C513's re-authorisation run (2026-10-08) | **The merge gate passes or fails on ONE case of thirteen, and the judge is non-deterministic.** C513's fresh run: catch **0.769** vs a 0.80 threshold (10 of 13 refuted; 11 needed), so the drain stays CLOSED. August scored 0.846 and passed BY ONE CASE. Six verdicts moved between the runs, three each way, on an IDENTICAL judgment fingerprint — model variance, not a code regression. One case is 7.7% of the rate: this denominator cannot support this threshold. Needs more labels or an acceptance that survives variance (best-of-N, a confidence interval). Day-log 2026-10-08. | Claude-fix | M | **CLOSED 2026-10-09 — the card asked for "more labels or an acceptance that survives variance"; the interval shows no acceptance survives at this n, so the answer IS the labels, with a number.** The catch denominator is **13** `different` cases, so the achievable values are 0.692, 0.769, **0.846**, 0.923, 1.0 — **0.80 is not among them**, the stated bar is really 11/13, and one case is 7.7 points. The 95% Wilson intervals are **[0.497, 0.918]** at 10/13 and **[0.578, 0.957]** at 11/13: they overlap by 0.34, so August's PASS and October's FAIL are statistically indistinguishable and `catch 0.769 < 0.8` is a statement about the denominator, not about the refuter. Measuring a threshold of this kind to ±0.05 needs about **196** different-cases. Best-of-N would cut the per-case noise and cannot fix 13; a confidence interval at n=13 has a floor near 0.5, which authorises nothing. BUILT: `b4b_refuter_gate.resolution()` + `wilson()` record achievability, step, CI, and the n required INTO every artifact (and backfilled into the 10-07 one, derived from its own rates — nothing re-judged), and `refuter_measured()`'s message — the string the journal and the era record quote — now ends *"the set is the work, not the rate"*. `passed` is UNTOUCHED, `CATCH_MIN` is still the ruling-pending 0.80, and the drain still fails closed: the safe state is unchanged and a test asserts the bar did not move. 9 tests. Lesson #68f01c. |

No live caller asks that way (`disposition_for` passes a host, `base.py` passes the URL), so the risk was latent. DONE: `nzme.py` writes the host with the measurement in its comment, and `ops/relabel_source_to_host.py` — guarded (every target must HAVE a policy row, zero nulls, exactly one host per label), idempotent, and reversible from a manifest carrying the exact reverse statements. Applied 650,210 + 242 (`https://www.nbr.co.nz/`) + 31 (`thestandard.org.nz`→`thestandard.nz`), then 73 stragglers from the 06:00 `nzme-archive-backfill` run that was in flight with the old constant loaded — `backwalk` wraps the module and `run_source` reads `mod.SOURCE`, so the fix covers that lane too. **Held intact: all 650,283 still `pipeline_status='held'`.** C530's gate now ASSERTS that from the label — the test that documented "nothing can check this" is the check — and its ceilings are rewritten. RESIDUE, a different job: the 54 that remain are 9 hosts with NO policy row at all (merch.opportunity 56→, ashburtondc, mbie, tewaihanga, umbraco, palfree, nzloyal, companiesoffice, www.comcom), which is adoption + a robots read, and C547's clock cannot see them because it walks the table and they have no row in it. A detector whose answer nothing acts on is a counter, not a filter. **Abstentions 20 → 15, off-roll-unknown 19 → 14.** The filter is applied in `windows()` and NOT in `universe()`: filtering the universe first took abstentions to 14 and broke THREE filed labels, because the prose carries the alias inside it — `ORNZas the abbreviation of the name of Outdoor Recreation NZ` is exactly how ORNZ resolves. So the row keeps contributing names and stops claiming to be a party. 5 new tests (22 in the suite), each half seen to fail. **WHY THE FULL NUMBER STAYS WITHHELD:** 14 parties are off today's roll with NO gazetted cancellation and 1 registered before the feed's reach, so their owed years are unknowable from the EC's own two publications — a permanent reason, not a to-do, and exactly #1's rule that a measurement which cannot be trusted is withheld with a reason rather than published with a caveat. Lesson #4ddc00. |
